To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed from the graph such that the two black nodes (E and J) are completely disconnected, we need to analyze the paths between these two nodes and identify the critical nodes whose removal will sever all possible paths.

1. **Identify Direct and Indirect Paths:**
   - Direct paths from E to J:
     - E-G-H-I-P-J
     - E-G-H-C-O-P-J
   - Indirect paths from E to J through intermediate nodes:
     - E-G-H-I-F-X-K-P-J
     - E-G-H-C-O-I-P-J
     - E-G-H-C-O-K-P-J
     - E-G-H-C-Y-N-B-L-F-X-K-P-J

2. **Identify Critical Nodes:**
   - Nodes that appear in multiple paths:
     - G, H, I, P are common in multiple paths.
     - Removing G, H, I, or P will disconnect several paths.
   - Intermediate nodes like F, X, K, C, O also play a role in connecting paths.

3. **Minimum Cut Set:**
   - Removing nodes G and H will disconnect almost all paths:
     - E-G-H-I-P-J is disconnected.
     - E-G-H-C-O-P-J is disconnected.
     - Other paths involving G and H are also disconnected.
   - However, there is still a path through E-F-X-K-P-J.
   - Removing node I will disconnect the remaining path through E-F-X-K-P-J.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes E and J is 2.

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [G, H]
